L1741: [REDACTED] (b) (4) Validation Report 8257531 did not meet FDA current guidelines for testing of accuracy, precision, selectivity, bench top stability at ambient room temperature, and freeze/thaw stability. L1743: Additional parameters evaluated in the partial validation were selectivity, bench top stability at ambient room temperature, and freeze/thaw stability. Room temperature stability was acceptable for at least 85 hours and 39 minutes and freeze/thaw stability was acceptable for up to 8 freeze/thaw cycles at -60°C to -80°C. Given that the additional validation parameters demonstrated acceptable performance, there was no negative impact on the ability to use the bioanalytical dataset.

L1751: ## 19.4.2. Population PK and PK-PD Analysis
L1752: The Applicant conducted a population pharmacokinetic (PopPK) and subsequently pharmacokinetic-pharmacodynamic (PK-PD) analysis to support the proposed dosing regimens of lebrikizumab for the treatment of adolescent and adult patients with moderate-to-severe atopic dermatitis (AD). Initially, a popPK model was developed to characterize the pharmacokinetics of lebrikizumab in patients and healthy and identify patient and laboratory factors significantly affecting its PK. PopPK analysis included data from 11 clinical studies that evaluated a dose range of 37.5 to 500 mg and involved 1607 subjects (healthy and patients) with a total of 6860 measurable concentrations. Both adult and adolescent patients were included with a body weight range of 40 to 192 kg.
L1753: The final PopPK model was a 2-compartment model with first-order absorption and linear elimination that adequately described concentration-time profiles of lebrikizumab. The steady state exposures (median Cmax, Ctrough, Cavg) were 2-fold higher following 250 mg Q2W regimen than those of Q4W, implying the linear PK characteristics of lebrikizumab. The steady state was attained after dose at Week 4. No covariates other than body weight (BW) were found to have significant impact on the lebrikizumab PK. The median Cavg,ss following 250 mg
L1754: Q2W dosing was 24% lower for the highest BW quartile while 33% higher for the lowest BW quartile compared to the population median. The impact of BW on lebrikizumab exposure is not clinically significant given the lack of dose-response relationship over a wider exposure range resulting from Q2W and Q4W treatment. PopPK estimated 17 - 21% higher exposure in adolescents than that of adults, which can be attributed to lower body weight distribution for adolescents. The PopPK model-estimated mean t_{1/2} was 24.5 days and bioavailability after SC dosing was 86.1%.
L1755: Subsequently, a PK-PD model based on the data from phase 2 and phase 3 studies was developed with an intent to support the clinical dose selection of lebrikizumab for the treatment of adult and adolescent patients with moderate-to-severe AD. This PK-PD analysis was conducted in two steps: i) development of a PopPK model to characterize the PK of lebrikizumab (described above) and ii) development of a PD model based on the exposures derived from the PopPK model in step 1 to describe the relationship between lebrikizumab exposure and Eczema Area and Severity Index (EASI) response. The combined population PK-PD model was also intended to understand and quantify patient factors which may affect EASI response.
L1756: The final PK-PD model was an indirect effect model that well described the longitudinal EASI response to lebrikizumab with or without topical corticosteroids (TCS) usage. The maximum effect (E_{max}) was estimated to be 0.831 (95% CI, 0.761 – 0.883) and the half maximal effective concentration (EC_{50}) was 16.5 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} (95% CI, 9.84 – 27.6 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}). There was a small effect of TCS use on the percentage change in EASI score from baseline. The model was adequate and validated to describe the observed EASI score, hence suitable to perform simulations in support of different dosing regimens. No covariates were identified as significant to impact exposure-response (E-R) relationship.
L1757: Simulations demonstrated that two loading doses of 500 mg lebrikizumab at Week 0 and 2 followed by 250 mg every two weeks (Q2W) up to Week 16 (induction period, last dose was at Week 14) produced rapid and greater responses compared to other dosing regimens with different loading doses. The response reached plateau after Week 16 for all dosing regimens. There was only a 3-4% higher response rate seen between Week 16 and 24 for 250 mg Q2W regimen compared to that of Week 16. Simulations of responders at Week 16 switching to maintenance regimens of 250 mg Q2W, Q4W, or placebo up to Week 52 indicated that the efficacy rates were comparable between Q2W and Q4W treatment regimen with a small numerically higher response rate gained from Q2W. The placebo withdrawal group maintained efficacy for long-term with a gradual and smaller decline over time. Overall, the (b) (4) dosing regimens of 500 mg lebrikizumab at Week 0 and 2 followed by 250 mg Q2W up to Week 16 (induction treatment) and thereafter either 250 mg Q2W or Q4W maintenance regimen for responders at Week 16 are supported by the PK-PD analysis.
L1758: ## BLA 761306 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Ebglyss (lebrikizumab) injection
L1759: Table 9295. Summary of PopPK analyses
L1760: General Information
Objectives of PopPK analysis	To characterize the pharmacokinetics (PK) of lebrikizumab in adult and adolescent participants with atopic dermatitis (AD) and healthy participants
To identify patient factors and laboratory parameters that may influence lebrikizumab disposition in this population, and
To derive individual PK parameters/exposure metrics for the future assessment of exposure-response relationships.

Study and population included	The PopPK analysis dataset contained data from 11 studies: 4 were in healthy participants and 7 were in participants with AD. The dataset includes a total of 1607 subjects from phase 1, 2, and 3 studies. Both adults and adolescents (12 to <18 years, ≥40 kg) were included in the phase 3 studies and included in the analysis. See Table 93 for details.
Dose(s) included	Single SC doses: 37.5 – 375 mg
Single IV or SC dose: 1 mg/kg
Multiple SC dose: 250 mg Q2W or Q4W preceded by 250 – 500 mg loading doses.

In phase 3 studies, the following doses and dosing regimen (b) (4) in the target patient population) were evaluated in adult and adolescent patients (12 to <18 years weighing at least 40 kg):Loading doses – 500 mg at Week 0 and Week 2
Induction doses – 250 mg every 2 weeks (Q2W) starting at Week 4 and continuing to Week 16
Maintenance doses – 250 mg Q2W or 250 mg every 4 weeks (Q4W) starting at Week 16 and continuing to Week 52

Refer to Table 93 for dosing regimen of individual study included in PopPK analysis.
PopPK analysis dataset	The source data from 11 studies contained 8050 post-baseline observations from 2015 participants. Based on criteria outlined in the analysis plan, 1190 observations were excluded from the source data.

Patients with AD: 1326
Number of PK samples: 6860
Total BLQs: None (All BLQs [N = 122] were excluded from the PopPK dataset)

L1762: ## Ebglyss (lebrikizumab) injection
L1763: individual variability (IIV) terms were included on Ka, CL, V2, and bioavailability (F1) parameters with omega blocks on CL-Ka and Ka-V2 terms and a logit transformation on F1 to restrict the estimate of bioavailability to between 0 and 1.
Body weight was included as a fixed exponent of 0.8 on clearance parameters (CL and Q) and a fixed exponent of 1 on volume parameters (V2 and V3).	
Base Model Parameters	Body weight was included as a covariate on CL, V2, V3, and Q in base model consistent with the previous modeling. See Table 95 for final parameter estimates as the base and final model are the same. The precision in parameter estimates is good. The 95% CI of parameter estimates obtained from bootstrap analysis also supports the precision of final parameter estimates.	Acceptable
Final Model Parameters	See Table 95 for parameter estimates.
An assessment of covariate effects was conducted using the Stepwise Covariate Modeling (SCM) procedure in PsN per the criteria outlined in the analysis plan. The covariates evaluated in the analysis are age, body weight, sex, race, disease state, injection location, markers of hepatic and renal function.
As noted, body weight was already included as a covariate on CL, V2, V3, and Q. Hence, BW was formally tested on absorption rate constant (Ka) only as a part of the SCM procedure.	Acceptable
GOF, VPC plots	The goodness-of-fit plots and prediction-corrected VPC plots of the final population PK model are shown in Figure 13, and 14, respectively.	Acceptable
Effect of Covariates	No covariates except body weight was found significant in PopPK model. Though BW was identified as a significant covariate, its effect on the PK is not clinically significant given the lack of dose-response relationship. Hence, no dose adjustment on the basis of body weight is needed for the adolescent (weight at least 40 kg) and adult patients with AD.	Acceptable

L1789: Table 9699. Summary of PK-PD analyses
L1790: General Information
Objectives of PopPK analysis	To characterize the relationship between lebrikizumab exposure and Eczema Area and Severity Index (EASI) response, based on data from the available phase 2 and phase 3 studies
To understand and quantify patient factors which may affect EASI response.
Overall, to support the clinical dose selection for lebrikizumab in adult and adolescent (aged 12 to <18 years, with body weight \ge40 kg) patients with moderate-to-severe AD and to support the dose recommendation for pediatric patients (<12 years) with AD

Study and population included	The PK-PD analysis dataset contained data from 5 studies: KGAH, KGAF (phase 2), KGAB, KGAC, KGAD (phase 3). The dataset includes a total of 1307 subjects. Both adults and adolescents (12 to <18 years, \ge40 kg) were included in the phase 3 studies and included in this analysis (see Table 93 for details). The study population consisted of 655 males and 652 females with weights ranging from 40 to 192 kg. The age range in the study population was 12 to 93 years, with 131 adolescents and 1176 adults.
Dose(s) included	125 and 250 mg (single or multiple doses)
PK-PD analysis dataset	The analyses included data up to 16 weeks of lebrikizumab treatment (induction dosing) and doses ranging from a single dose of 125 mg to 250 mg every 2 weeks (Q2W).
Covariates evaluated	Age, sex, body weight, race, and baseline IGA were tested on Emax and EC50 parameter. Additionally, all these covariates except baseline IGA were tested on Kout and Epbo.
L1791: Emax: maximal effect, EC50: half maximal effective concentration, Kout: first order rate constant for loss of response, Epbo: placebo effect
Final Model	Summary	Acceptability [FDA's Comments]
Software and version	The PK-PD analysis was carried out using NONMEM (Version 7.4.3, ICON Development Solutions, Elicott City, MD, USA) under Windows 10 and the GNU gfortran compiler (Version 4.6.0).
Post-processing of NONMEM analysis results was carried out in R version 4.0.5 (R Development Core Team, 2008).
NONMEM run execution and visual predictive checks (VPCs) were carried out using Perl-speaks-NONMEM (PsN), version 4.8.1.
Acceptable
Model Development	The PK-PD model was developed in a stepwise approach to assess potential model bias and/or misspecification as more data became available.
Preliminary models were built using phase 2 studies KGAG and KGAF, then redeveloped with the addition of phase 3 studies KGAB, KGAC, and KGAD
Model development was carried out using first order conditional estimation with interaction (FOCE-I), including individual pharmacokinetics (PK) estimates from the previously developed PopPK model
Exploratory analysis showed that there is a difference in baseline EASI score across studies, hence study effect on baseline EASI score was estimated by stratifying the studies into 3 groups: KGAG (mean baseline 25.4), KGAF and KGAD (mean baseline 26.8), and KGAB and KGAD (mean baseline 29.0)
Exploratory analysis also showed that TCS use contributed to a little higher decrease in the percent change of EASI score from baseline compared to that with no TCS use (Figure 15). Therefore, the model included a time-varying multiplicative TCS effect on the first-order rate constant for loss of response (Kout).
The model also included a logit fractional placebo effect on the zero-order constant for the production of response (Kin).
Subgroups of interest were explored graphically, including age, sex, body weight, race, and baseline Investigator's Global Assessment (IGA) (e.g., moderate vs severe) and tested as covariates in the PK-PD model as indicated by the results of the graphical analysis.
Acceptable
L1792: ## BLA 761306 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Ebglyss (lebrikizumab) injection
L1793: Model Parameters	PK parameters were estimated in a previous PopPK model and combined with the PK-PD analysis dataset. The estimated PD parameters are presented in Table 97.	Acceptable
GOF, VPC plots	The goodness-of-fit plots and prediction-corrected VPC plots of the final population PK-PD model are shown in Figure 16 and 17, respectively. The final EASI model (Run 857) demonstrated good agreement between predicted and observed data. The condition number was low (33.71), and all parameters were well estimated with acceptable precision. Plots of the conditional weighted residuals show that the model is overall unbiased over time and across the concentration range.	Acceptable
Significant covariates	The final model only included two covariates to be significant: TCS effect on Kout and study effects on baseline EASI. All other continuous or categorical covariates were found insignificant. Body weight was included in the PopPK model and was re-evaluated for its potential effect on Emax or EC50 in the PK-PD model but was not found significant.

L1804: ## Applicant's Summary of Pharmacokinetic Results
L1805: ## Brief description of the PopPK model results:
L1806: The Applicant developed a 2-compartment model with first-order absorption and linear elimination to characterize the PK profile of lebrikizumab based on 11 pooled studies. The model was found adequate and predictive based on GOF and VPC plots, which was supported by the reviewer's independent analysis of the model (see **Table 92** and hyperlinked model results and plots). Only body weight was identified as a significant covariate on lebrikizumab PK. Age, sex, race, injection site location, disease (atopic dermatitis vs healthy), and markers of hepatic and renal function were found as insignificant covariates. Note that base model included BW-based allometric exponents on clearance and volume of parameters. This base model was considered as the final model as all other covariates tested were insignificant.
L1807: Note that immunogenicity was not tested as a covariate in the current PopPK model since the Week 52 data were not available at the time of this analysis. Immunogenicity will therefore be evaluated in a separate analysis.
L1808: Lebrikizumab concentrations were decreased with increase in BW, which is consistent with the expected PK of a monoclonal antibody. Lebrikizumab bioavailability after subcutaneous administration was 86.1% as estimated in the final model. The mean half-life of lebrikizumab was 24.5 days.
L1809: ## PK results based on PopPK model analyses:
L1810: The Applicant conducted simulation using the final PopPK model, which provided the following PK results:
L1811: - Steady state lebrikizumab concentrations were rapidly achieved after 250 mg dosing at Week 4, with the 500 mg loading doses at Weeks 0 and 2.
- Maximum concentration over a dosing interval at steady state (C_{max,ss}), average concentration over a dosing interval at steady state (C_{avg,ss}), and minimum concentration over a dosing interval at steady state (C_{trough,ss}) following 250 mg every 2 weeks (Q2W) were 108, 100, and 87 \mug/mL, respectively. C_{max,ss}, C_{avg,ss}, and C_{trough,ss} following 250 mg every 4 weeks (Q4W) were 63, 51, and 36 \mug/mL, respectively. These simulated exposure metrics indicate that lebrikizumab concentrations were approximately 50% lower for 250 mg Q4W dosing compared to 250 mg Q2W (**Table 98**).
- The median lebrikizumab C_{avg,ss} is approximately 50% lower for the 250 mg Q4W dosing group compared to the 250 mg Q2W group in the maintenance period. Overlap in lebrikizumab concentrations was observed between Q2W and Q4W.
- Higher BW resulted in lower overall lebrikizumab concentrations.
- At steady state after 250 mg Q2W dosing, simulations showed that the highest and lowest BW quartiles have median C_{avg,ss} of 75.7 and 133 \mug/mL, compared to the overall population median C_{avg,ss} of 100 \mug/mL (**Table 98** and **Table 99**). Therefore, the C_{avg,ss} was reduced by about 24% for the highest BW quartile and increased about 33% for the lowest BW quartile.
- At steady state after 250 mg Q4W dosing, simulations showed that the highest and lowest BW quartiles have median C_{avg,ss} of 37.9 and 64.9 \mug/mL, compared to the overall population median C_{avg,ss} of 51.1 \mug/mL (**Table 98** and **Table 99**). Therefore, the C_{avg,ss} was reduced by about 26% for the highest BW quartile and increased about 27% for the lowest BW quartile.
- The simulated median C_{trough,ss} values were above 50 \mug/mL for the Q2W regimen for all BW quartiles and generally 25 to 50 \mug/mL for the Q4W regimen for all BW quartiles. Therefore, trough concentrations were maintained above the drug concentration that produces 50% of maximum effect (EC_{50}) of 16.5 \mug/mL (estimated by exposure-response model), even with the less frequent dosing of Q4W.

L1824: ## Reviewer's PopPK analysis
L1825: The reviewer's own analysis of the final PopPK model provided similar parameters estimates with similar precision as reported by the Applicant. The adequacy and predictability of the Applicant's PopPK model is supported by the reviewer's independent analysis. The summary results of the final PopPK model were provided in **Table 92**, hence will not be repeated here.
L1826: It is noted that body weight was identified as significant covariate. The clearance of lebrikizumab is increased with the increase in BW. This was supported by exploratory analysis of lebrikizumab concentrations-time data derived from phase 3 study. For example, predose concentrations (Ctrough) observed at Week 16 of phase 3 studies were decreased with increase in BW as seen in the **Figure 18**.
L1827: On the other hand, evaluation of markers of renal function (defined by estimated glomerular filtration rate, eGFR) as a continuous covariate was not a significant covariate. However, exploratory analysis of renal function indicates that 40 patients with moderate renal function
L1828: (30 mL/min/1.73 m2 &lt; eGFR &lt; 60 mL/min/1.73 m2) were included in phase 2 and 3 studies. Only 21 of 40 patients with moderate renal impairment (RI) were available within phase 3 studies. For these 21 moderate RI patients, the median Ctrough at Week 16 was 73.6 µg/mL, which was approximately 22% lower than that of population median (94.4 µg/mL). However, a 22% decrease in lebrikizumab exposure in patients with moderate RI is unlikely to be clinically significant.
L1829: Figure 18. Lebrikizumab observed Week 16 predose (trough) concentrations by baseline body weight: all phase 3 participants (adults and adolescents)
L1830: ## Summary of PK results based on reviewer's simulation
L1831: The reviewer has performed simulation using the final PopPK model to describe the PK characteristics of lebrikizumab under different scenarios. For example, the phase 3 dosing regimens, lebrikizumab 250 mg Q2W up to Week 16 (with/without loading doses) followed by 250 mg Q2W, 250 mg Q4W(b)(4) up to Week 52 were considered as relevant scenarios to describe the PK parameters of lebrikizumab.
L1832: Since BW was a significant covariate, the BW was sampled from the observed baseline BW data of phase 3 studies. The reviewer's simulation-based PK results supported the Applicant's summary of PK results as stated above.
L1833: PK simulation shows that steady state is attained rapidly when two loading doses of 500 mg lebrikizumab are used at Week 0 and 2 compared to that with no loading dose. The steady state was achieved after Week 4 dosing (250 mg lebrikizumab) with loading doses while it took more than 12 weeks to reach steady state without loading doses (Figure 19).

The median lebrikizumab C_{\text{avg,ss}} is approximately 50% lower for the 250 mg Q4W dosing group compared to the 250 mg Q2W group in the maintenance period. There is an overlap in PK exposure between Q2W and Q4W regimen (Table 100 and Figure 20).

L1850: ## Exposure-Response Analyses
L1851: To allow for adequate exposure-response analysis, the Applicant developed a PK-PD model as summarized in **Table 96**. The model was determined as adequate and predictive model. The model's validity was supported by the agreement between observed and simulated EASI response after longer term dosing, even though only data up to Week 16 were used in the model development. Hence, it is reasonable to use the PK-PD model for simulation of E-R relationship for different dosing scenarios. To support the Sponsor's proposed dose recommendations, simulations were performed to describe E-R relationship using various EASI responses as endpoints for different dosing regimen and treatment duration. The Applicant's E-R analysis has been summarized below.
L1852: Applicant's summary of E-R analysis based on PK-PD model:
L1853: - The longitudinal EASI response to lebrikizumab exposure was well described by the final PK-PD indirect effect model which included a drug effect, placebo effect, and time-varying TCS effect. The model-estimated half maximal effective concentration (EC50) was 16.5 µg/mL (95% CI, 9.84 - 27.6 µg/mL). The effect of TCS usage (whether transitory or consistent) was small. Without TCS, the EASI turnover rate was 19.1 days compared to 17.7 days with TCS usage. The model did not identify any significant covariates affecting E-R relationship; hence no dose adjustment based on extrinsic or intrinsic factors is recommended.
- The typical baseline EASI score varied across five studies with the lowest baseline observed for KGAG (25.4, 95% CI 14.4 - 36.4), the next highest for patients in the KGAF and KGAD studies (26.8, 95% CI 15.8 - 37.8), and the highest baseline in patients in the KGAB and KGAC studies (29.0, 95% CI 18 - 40). A study effect on baseline was stratified into these three groups.
- No significant covariates were identified by the PK-PD model. Note that BW was also reevaluated in the PK-PD model but was not found to be a statistically significant covariate.
- The final PK-PD model was used to simulate different dosing scenarios with or without daily TCS use to illustrate dose-response during induction period and maintenance period up to Week 52 to support the proposed dosing regimens. Eczema Area and Severity Index (EASI) 50, 75, and 90 (EASI 50, EASI 75, EASI 90) and percent change in EASI from baseline were used as the response variables. Simulation showed that induction regimen of 500 mg lebrikizumab at Week 0, 2, then 250 mg Q2W up to Week 16 (last induction dose was at Week 14) predicted to have achieved a high level of efficacy with rapid onset compared to other induction regimens with or without TCS use (**Figure 25**). This induction regimen as proposed by the Applicant is reasonable. Furthermore, simulation up to Week 24 of maintenance treatment with continued induction dosing regimen showed that EASI 75 and 90 curves start to flatten for all regimens after Week 16. Continued 250 mg Q2W regimen would only provide approximately 3 – 4% more patients achieving EASI 75 or 90 between Week 16 and 24. EASI 75 and 90 response rates were numerically higher for Q2W regimen compared to that of Q4W.
- The estimated effect of concomitant use of TCS was small compared to the lebrikizumab effect. At Week 16 for the 250 mg Q2W regimen, the percentage of patients achieving EASI 75 was estimated as 66% for lebrikizumab monotherapy, compared with 70% for lebrikizumab with daily TCS use. The percentage of patients achieving EASI 90 was estimated as 34% for lebrikizumab monotherapy, compared with 37% for lebrikizumab with daily TCS use. Therefore, the incremental effect of daily TCS use was approximately 3% to 4% for EASI 75 and 90 (**Figure 25 and Table 101A-B**).

L1862: - Though the PK-PD model included data up to Week-16, the simulation of maintenance treatment up to Week 52 was performed using various dosing regimens to predict other clinical trial data. This can also serve as an external validation of the PK-PD model. The simulations of patients who responded at Week-16 after induction period and subsequently received 250 mg Q2W or Q4W or withdrew to placebo during maintenance period well described the observed data as presented in **Figure 26**. However, a slight model underprediction of lebrikizumab efficacy was seen for placebo withdrawal. To note, responders at Week 16 were those who had an IGA score of 0 or 1 or, at least a 75% reduction in EASI score from baseline to Week 16. Additionally, the simulations didn't account for TCS use, which was likely used in placebo withdrawal group, although a relatively small effect on the efficacy is expected from TCS use.

L1884: The reviewer also conducted simulations using different maintenance regimens to assess the model's ability to predict EASI response to Week 52. The induction regimen (500 mg at Week 0, 2, then 250 mg Q2W up to Week 16) was the same proposed by the Applicant. L1891: ## Reviewer's Comments
L1892: *The Applicant's PopPK and PK-PD models were adequate to describe the PK of lebrikizumab and exposure-response relationship, hence suitable for simulating different scenarios. Based on PK-PD simulations, the induction treatment with 500 mg lebrikizumab at Week 0 and 2 (loading doses) followed by 250 mg Q2W up to Week 16 attained higher efficacy with its rapid onset compared to other induction regimens. Subsequent simulations using different maintenance dosing regimens showed comparable effectiveness of 250 mg Q2W and Q4W regimens starting from Week 16. All endpoints (e.g., EASI 50, 75, 90, and percent change in EASI score from baseline) included in the E-R analysis supports both Q2W and Q4W as potential maintenance treatment regimens.*
L1893: *The reviewer noted that the Applicant's predicted percentage change of EASI score versus time plots demonstrated a narrow confidence interval as opposed to the reviewer's CI estimated based on lower 2.5% and upper 97.5% percentiles cutoff of simulated data. The reason for discrepancy is unclear but it does not affect the overall trend of ER relationship.*
L1894: ## Conclusion
L1895: The final PopPK model adequately described concentration-time profiles of lebrikizumab. The steady state exposures (median Cmax, Ctrough, Cavg) were 2-fold higher following 250 mg Q2W regimen than those of Q4W, implying the linear PK characteristics of lebrikizumab. No covariates other than body weight were found to have significant impact on the lebrikizumab PK. The median Cavg,ss following 250 mg Q2W dosing was 24% lower for the highest BW quartile while 33% higher for the lowest BW quartile compared to the population median. The impact of BW on lebrikizumab exposure is not clinically significant given the lack of dose-response relationship over a wider exposure range resulting from Q2W and Q4W treatment.
L1896: PopPK estimated 17 - 21% higher exposure in adolescents than that of adults, which can be attributed to lower body distribution for adolescents. The PopPK model estimated mean t_{1/2} was 24.5 days and bioavailability after SC dosing was 85%.
L1897: The final PK-PD model was an indirect effect model that well described the longitudinal EASI response to lebrikizumab with or without topical corticosteroids (TCS) usage. The maximum effect (E_{max}) was estimated to be 0.831 (95% CI, 0.761 – 0.883) and the half maximal effective concentration (EC_{50}) was 16.5 \mug/mL (95% CI, 9.84 – 27.6 \mug/mL). There was a small effect of TCS use on the percentage change in EASI score from baseline. The model was adequate and validated to describe the observed EASI score, hence suitable to perform simulations in support of different dosing regimens. No covariates were identified as significant to impact exposure-response (E-R) relationship.
L1898: Simulations demonstrated that two loading doses of 500 mg lebrikizumab at Week 0 and 2 followed by 250 mg every two weeks (Q2W) up to Week 16 (induction period, last dose was at Week 14) produced rapid and greater responses compared to other dosing regimens with different loading doses. The response reached plateau after Week 16 for all dosing regimens. There were only a 3-4% higher response rates seen between Week 16 and 24 for 250 mg Q2W regimen. Simulations of responders at Week 16 switching to maintenance regimens of 250 mg Q2W, Q4W, or placebo up to Week 52 indicated that the efficacy rates were comparable between Q2W and Q4W treatment regimen with a small numerically higher rates in favor of Q2W. The placebo withdrawal group maintained efficacy for long-term with a gradual and smaller decline over time. Overall, the (b) (4) dosing regimens of 500 mg lebrikizumab at Week 0 and 2 followed by 250 mg Q2W up to Week 16 (induction treatment) and thereafter either 250 mg Q2W or Q4W maintenance regimen for responders at Week 16 are supported by the PK-PD analysis.
L1899: ## 19.5. Additional Clinical Outcome Assessment Analyses

L1908: Figure 4 is a flowchart illustrating the study design for Studies KGAB and KGAC, divided into Induction and Maintenance phases.
Induction Phase:
Screening leads to Randomization (n=400, 2:1).
Randomization groups: Lebrikizumab (Loading dose + 250 mg Q2W) and Placebo (Q2W).
Baseline measurement occurs before Week 16.
At Week 16, subjects are Re-Randomized (2:1) into Responders and Non-Responders.
Maintenance Phase:
Responders are randomized into three arms: Lebrikizumab 250 mg Q2W, Lebrikizumab 250 mg Q4W, and Placebo.
Non-Responders are assigned to the Escape Arm: LEB 250 mg Q2W.
At Week 24, EASI-50 Non-Responders are switched to the Escape Arm (LEB 250 mg Q2W).
At Week 32, EASI-50 Non-Responders are discontinued.
At Week 40, EASI-50 Non-Responders are discontinued.
At Week 48, EASI-50 Non-Responders are discontinued.
At Week 52, the study concludes with a Long-Term Extension Study or Safety Follow-up Visit, leading to Discontinue (D/C).
L1909: Figure 5 is a timeline diagram illustrating the study design for Study KGAD.
The timeline shows the Treatment Perioda from Week 0 to Week 16, coprimary endpoints IGA (0,1)f,g,h and EASI-75h.
Screening leads to Randomization 2:1b.
During the Treatment Period:
Group 1: LDc (Loading Dose) followed by LEB 250 mg Q2W + TCS.
Group 2: PBO + TCS.
After Week 16, the study proceeds to Study KGAA LTE or safety follow-upd.

L1911: Figure 7 displays two graphical approaches for controlling the type 1 error rate across four endpoints (IGA 01, EASI-75, Pruritus NRS-4, and Sleep Loss-2) measured at Weeks 4 (W4), 16 (W16), and 2 (W2).
The left diagram (KGAB/KGAC) shows a hierarchical structure where the IGA 01 W16 endpoint is tested first. If it fails, the remaining alpha level (0.05) is recycled and split among the subsequent endpoints (EASI-75 W16, Pruritus NRS-4 W16, and Sleep Loss-2 W16). The alpha levels allocated are 0.01, 0.04, and 0.5, respectively. If IGA 01 W16 passes, the alpha level is recycled and split among the four endpoints at the bottom of the hierarchy (IGA 01 W4, Pruritus NRS-4 W4, Sleep Loss-2 W16, and Pruritus NRS-4 W2) with alpha levels 0.1, 0.99, 0.4, and 0.5, respectively. The Pruritus NRS-4 W2 endpoint is tested last, with an alpha level of 0.01.
The right diagram (KGAD) shows a simpler sequential structure where the IGA 01 W16 endpoint is tested first. If it fails, the remaining alpha level (0.05) is recycled and split among the subsequent endpoints (EASI-75 W16, Pruritus NRS-4 W16, and EASI-75 & Pruritus NRS-4 W16). The alpha levels allocated are 0.01, 0.04, and 0.5, respectively. If IGA 01 W16 passes, the alpha level is recycled and split among the four endpoints at the bottom of the hierarchy (IGA 01 W4, Pruritus NRS-4 W4, Sleep Loss-2 W16, and Pruritus NRS-4 W2) with alpha levels 0.1, 0.99, 0.4, and 0.5, respectively. The Pruritus NRS-4 W2 endpoint is tested last, with an alpha level of 0.01.

L1914: Line graph showing the proportion of responders (IGA 0 or 1 and a ≥ 2-point improvement from baseline) over time (Analysis Visit in Week) for three groups: PBO (LEB Withdrawal), LEB 250mg Q2W, and LEB 250mg Q4W. The Y-axis is Proportion of Responders (%), ranging from 0 to 100. The X-axis is Analysis Visit (Week), ranging from 16 to 52. At Week 16, all groups start at 100%. By Week 52, the proportions are: PBO (LEB Withdrawal) 49.8%, LEB 250mg Q2W 64.6%, and LEB 250mg Q4W 80.6%. The LEB 250mg Q4W group maintains the highest proportion of responders throughout the study period.
L1915: Line graph showing the proportion of responders (subjects achieving EASI75 at Week 16) who maintain the EASI75 response over time (Analysis Visit in Week) for Study KGAC. The Y-axis is 'Proportion of Responders (%)' (0 to 100). The X-axis is 'Analysis Visit (Week)' (16, 20, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 44, 48, 52). Three lines are plotted: PBO (LEB Withdrawal), LEB 250mg Q2W, and LEB 250mg Q4W. At Week 16, all groups start at 100%. By Week 52, the PBO group drops to 72.0%. The LEB 250mg Q2W group drops to 77.4%. The LEB 250mg Q4W group drops to 84.7%. The LEB 250mg Q4W group consistently maintains the highest proportion of responders across all visits.
L1916: Histogram showing the PK Sampling Distribution (All Studies). The X-axis represents Days (0 to 616), and the Y-axis represents the Number of PK samples (0 to 1800). The distribution is highly skewed towards lower days, with the highest frequency of samples occurring around 0 to 14 days, followed by a sharp decline.
L1917: Figure 13 displays four Goodness-of-Fit (GoF) plots for the Final Population PK Model. The plots are arranged in a 2x2 grid:
Top Left: Observed concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}) vs. Individual predicted concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}). The data points generally follow the diagonal line, indicating a good fit.
Top Right: Observed concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}) vs. Population Predicted Concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}). The data points generally follow the diagonal line, indicating a good fit.
Bottom Left: Conditional Weighted Residuals vs. Population predicted concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}). The residuals are scattered randomly around the horizontal line at 0, suggesting no systematic bias.
Bottom Right: Conditional Weighted Residuals vs. Time after dose (Day). The residuals are scattered randomly around the horizontal line at 0, suggesting no systematic bias over time.
L1918: Figure 14 displays the Prediction-Corrected Visual Predictive Check (VPC) for the Final Population PK model. The Y-axis is logarithmic, labeled "Observations (Pred Corr)", ranging from 0.1 to 100. The X-axis is labeled "Time from first dose (days)", ranging from 0 to approximately 180 days.
The plot shows observed data points (blue diamonds) and prediction-corrected intervals (red dashed lines and shaded areas). The observed data points generally fall within the predicted intervals, indicating a good fit of the model to the data.
L1919: Visual Predictive Check (VPC) plot titled 'Visual Predictive Check Observations vs. Time'. The x-axis is 'Time from previous dose (days)' ranging from 0 to 30. The y-axis is 'Observations (Pred Corr)' on a logarithmic scale from 0.1 to 100. Blue dots represent observed data. A solid red line depicts the median observed data. Pink shaded areas define the 95% confidence intervals around the median of the simulated data. Red dashed lines represent the 5th and 95th percentiles of observed data. Blue shaded areas represent the simulated 95% confidence interval of the same.

L1922: Figure 17 displays five Prediction-Corrected Visual Predictive Checks (VPCs) for the Logit EASI Score over Nominal Time (Days) for five studies: TREBLE, AD-01, KGAC, KGAB, and KGAD. Each plot shows observed data points (blue circles) with vertical error bars, a solid black line representing the median, dashed red lines representing the 5th and 95th percentiles of the observed data, and shaded areas indicating the 95% Confidence Interval (CI) around the median (blue area) and the 5th and 95th percentiles of the simulated response (pink areas). The plots generally show a decreasing trend in Logit EASI Score over time, consistent with treatment effect.
L1923: Week 16 Concentration by Body Weight in Phase 3 Studies
Population: ADOLESCENT (Red dots), ADULT (Blue dots)
This scatter plot displays the Week 16 Trough Serum Concentration (µg/mL) on the Y-axis (ranging from 0 to 275) versus Body Weight at Study Entry (Year) on the X-axis (ranging from 50 to 175). The data points are categorized by population (Adolescent and Adult). A fitted curve (blue line) and a shaded confidence interval are shown, illustrating a negative correlation between body weight and trough concentration.
L1924: Figure 19 displays two plots (A and B) showing Lebrikizumab Concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}) versus Time (Weeks from first dose) for virtual patient simulations to 52 weeks. The simulations compare two dosing regimens: 2 x 500 mg Loading Dose (LD) followed by 250 mg Q2W (violet/red) or 250 mg Q4W (blue) maintenance dosing, versus No LD followed by 250 mg Q2W (violet/red) or 250 mg Q4W (blue) maintenance dosing.
Plot A: 250 mg Q2W Maintenance Dosing. The concentration stabilizes around 100 \mu\text{g/mL} by Week 16 (indicated by a dashed line). The concentration range is approximately 50 to 150 \mu\text{g/mL}.
Plot B: 250 mg Q4W Maintenance Dosing. The concentration stabilizes around 50 \mu\text{g/mL} by Week 16. The concentration range is approximately 0 to 100 \mu\text{g/mL}.
The plots illustrate that the Q2W regimen achieves a higher steady-state concentration than the Q4W regimen.
L1925: Figure 20 is a line graph titled "Lebri Phase 3 PK Simulations to 52 Weeks". The Y-axis represents Lebrikizumab Concentration (\mug/mL) from 0 to 150. The X-axis represents Time (Weeks from first dose) from 0 to 52. A vertical dashed line marks Week 16, separating the Induction (Q2W) period from the Maintenance (Q2W or Q4W) period.
The graph compares two dosing regimens: Q2W (Quarterly, represented by a pink line) and Q4W (Quarterly, represented by a blue line). Both regimens show a rapid increase in concentration during the induction phase (Weeks 0-16). During the maintenance phase (Weeks 16-52), the Q2W regimen maintains a higher median concentration (around 100 \mug/mL) compared to the Q4W regimen (around 70 \mug/mL). Shaded areas represent the 5th and 95th percentiles of simulated values at each time point.
L1926: Simulated Week 52 Trough Concentrations by Regimen and Weight Quartile
Box plot showing Lebrikizumab Concentrations (\mu\text{g/mL}) versus Weight Quartile (Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4) for two dosing regimens: Q2W (left panel) and Q4W (right panel).
The Y-axis represents Lebrikizumab Concentrations (\mu\text{g/mL}), ranging from 0 to 200. The X-axis represents Weight Quartile (Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4).
For the Q2W regimen, concentrations generally decrease as weight quartile increases. For the Q4W regimen, concentrations are significantly lower across all weight quartiles compared to Q2W.
Individual data points (dots) are shown outside the whiskers, representing values beyond \pm 1.5 times the interquartile range.

L1929: Figure 25 displays four graphs showing the predicted percentage of EASI responders over time (weeks) for various induction dosing regimens, comparing monotherapy (no TCS) and monotherapy with TCS (top row for EASI 75, bottom row for EASI 90).
The Regimen legend defines four dosing strategies:
500/250 mg Q2W (Green line)
500/250 mg Q4W (Light Blue line)
250/125 mg Q4W (Purple line)
Placebo (Dark Blue line)
The graphs illustrate that higher dose regimens (500/250 mg Q2W and 500/250 mg Q4W) achieve higher percentages of EASI 75 and EASI 90 responders faster and to a greater extent than lower dose regimens (250/125 mg Q4W) or placebo, especially when combined with TCS use.
Regimen refers to the loading dose followed by the treatment regimen. 500/250 mg Q2W: two loading doses of 500 mg Q2W followed by 250 mg Q2W; 500/250 mg Q4W: single loading dose of 500 mg followed by 250 mg Q4W; 250/125 mg Q2W: single loading dose of 250 mg followed by 125 mg Q4W.

The legend indicates:
LEBRI 250 mg Q2W (Purple line/shaded region)
LEBRI 250 mg Q4W (Blue line/shaded region)
LEBRI Withdrawal (Placebo) (Gray line/shaded region)
LS Mean \pm SEM (Black dots and lines)
The top two graphs show the EASI Change from Baseline (%) for the induction phase (Weeks 0 to 16) and the maintenance phase (Weeks 16 to 52). The bottom graph shows the EASI Change from Baseline (%) for the maintenance phase (Weeks 16 to 52) for all three groups.
L1931: Figure 27 displays simulated dose-response curves for lebrikizumab for EASI 50, EASI 75, and EASI 90, comparing monotherapy (No TCS) and combination therapy (With TCS) across three dosing regimens (500/250 mg Q2W, 500/250 mg Q4W, 250/125 mg Q4W) and placebo (Placebo). The X-axis represents Time (Weeks) from 0 to 25. The Y-axis represents the Predicted % responders (EASI 50, EASI 75, or EASI 90).
The legend indicates the Regimen (500/250 mg Q2W, 500/250 mg Q4W, 250/125 mg Q4W) and Frequency (Q2W, Q4W, Placebo).
For EASI 50 (Top Left): All active regimens show high response rates (around 75-80%) by Week 24, regardless of TCS use. Placebo remains low (around 50%).
For EASI 75 (Top Right): All active regimens show high response rates (around 60-65%) by Week 24, regardless of TCS use. Placebo remains low (around 40%).
For EASI 90 (Bottom Left): All active regimens show moderate response rates (around 35-40%) by Week 24, regardless of TCS use. Placebo remains low (around 15%).
The curves generally show that Q2W regimens achieve slightly higher response rates than Q4W regimens, and the addition of TCS slightly increases the response rate for active treatments.
L1932: Figure showing three graphs comparing EASI Change from Baseline (%) over Weeks from first dose (0 to 52) for three treatment groups: Lebri 250 mg Q2W, Lebri 250 mg Q4W, and Lebri Withdrawal (Placebo). The Y-axis ranges from 0 to -100%. The solid black line indicates the median, and the shaded blue area indicates the 95% CI for simulated % change of EASI from baseline. The line with dots represents the observed LS mean with SEM.
L1933: Figure 29 displays two graphs showing the predicted percentage of responders achieving EASI 75 (left) and EASI 90 (right) over Time (Weeks) from 0 to 50. The Y-axis represents the Predicted %EASI75 responders or Predicted %EASI90 responders. The X-axis represents Time (Weeks). The legend indicates three Regimens: 500/250 mg Q2W without TCS (Blue line), 500/250 mg Q4W without TCS (Red line), and Placebo without TCS (Green line). Both active regimens show high predicted responder rates (around 65-70% for EASI 75 and 35-40% for EASI 90) by Week 52, with Q2W slightly higher than Q4W. The placebo group shows significantly lower responder rates (around 25% for EASI 75 and 15% for EASI 90).
